One approach: Use "damped Newton / gradient descent" in log domain. Let x_i = log a_i. R(x) defined; scale invariant so R independent of adding constant to x. Fix mean zero or max. Use projected? Since need nonnegative but log handles. We can optimize on subspace mean zero. Gradient:

∂R/∂a_i = (w_i M? no) = (S^2 ∂M -2M S)/S^4 = (1/S)(∂M/∂a_i /S - 2M/S^2?) Actually ∂R = M' /S^2 -2M/S^3. So ∂R/∂log a_i = a_i M' /S^2 -2M/S.
Let q_i = a_i M' /S^2. Then dR/dx_i = q_i - 2M/S = q_i - 2R.
Where M'=∇w_k ∂c_k/∂a_i. q_i = ∇_k w_k a_i a_{k-i}/S^2. If w uniform over all k with sum? For R=M/S^2, if active weights proportional to c_k? If approximate smooth M by softmax, weights w_k proportional exp(βc_k). Then q_i is expected product contributions. Gradient = q_i - 2R.

Let's define:
```
def _next_power_two(x):
    return 1 << (int(x)-1).bit_length()
```
Top-level.

`fast_score(a, nfft=None)`:
- a = np.asarray(a,float)
- maybe clip 0? We'll keep nonnegative.
- s = a.sum(); if s<=1e-12 return inf
- a = a/s
- if nfft is None: nfft = _next_power_two(2*a.size-1)
- f = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
- conv = np.fft.irfft(f*f, nfft)[:2*n-1]
- # due roundoff, maybe small negative; max_real = np.max(conv)
- if not finite return inf
- return float(2*n*max_real)
```

Helper `fft_conv(a, n, nfft)`:
```
def fft_conv(a,n,nfft):
    f=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
    return np.fft.irfft(f*f,n=nfft)[:2*n-1]
```
Could ensure real. Since a normalized positive, conv positive; small negative set to 0? Max unaffected. Use `conv = np.ascontiguousarray`. For performance, if nfft cached.

`fast_score_from_conv(c, n)`:
```
m=c.max()
return 2*n*m if m>0 else inf
```
If m is tiny negative due all zeros, inf.

Line search `try_step(a, d, base, factors, n, nfft, current_score, best)`:
- For each factor in descending maybe:
```
lam = factor*base
trial = a + lam*d
if trial.min()<0: trial = np.maximum(trial,0)
s = trial.sum()
if s<=1e-15: continue
trial /= s
c = fft_conv(trial,n,nfft)
sc = 2*n*c.max()
if sc < current_score*(1-1e-12): accept best, return trial,sc
```
We can also accept slightly worse? No, purely descent. If none, after many fails, perturb.

def active_derivative(a, K, n, nfft_g):

But implementing both. Direct:
```
g=np.zeros(n); r=a[::-1]
for k in K:
  i_lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); i_hi=min(n-1,k); L=i_hi-i_lo+1; start=n-1-k+i_lo
  g[i_lo:i_hi+1] += r[start:start+L]
g /= len(K)
```
FFT:
```
w=np.zeros(2*n-1); w[K]=1/len(K)
nfft_g=nextpow(3*n-2)
fr=np.fft.rfft(r,nfft_g); fw=np.fft.rfft(w,nfft_g)
conv=np.fft.irfft(fr*fw,nfft_g)
g=conv[n-1:n-1+n]
```
Note orientation? We used r*w. Yes.

Set `GS_SHAPE`? We can use one function for piecewise linear. Need control count m. Global `GS_M`, `GS_N`, `GS_NFFT`, `GS_BOUNDS` maybe.

```
def piece_score(theta):
    n = GS_N
    m = GS_M
    if theta is None or len(theta)!=m: return np.inf
    y = np.empty(m)
    y[0]=1.0
    if m>1:
       y[1:] = np.exp(np.clip(theta[1:], -12, 8))
    x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    yk = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    a = np.interp(x, yk, y, left=y[0], right=y[-1])
    # normalize to S=1? fast_score does, but for score we can directly call fast_score(a)
    return fast_score(a, n, GS_NFFT)
```
But `np.interp` per eval O(n), okay. Can precompute x? Global `GS_X`, `GS_YK` to avoid linspace each eval. Set before optimization. `piece_score` uses them. Good.

But anchoring y[0]=1 may prevent shapes with first point exactly 0? exp lower. okay.

Bounds for theta[1:]: maybe [-8, 6]. Use `bounds = [(0,0)] + [(-8,6)]*(m-1)`. Since theta first fixed; L-BFGS x0 length m-1? Could optimize θ of length m-1 (deviations) and ignore first. Better `piece_score_dev(theta)` length m-1. Global. Then x0 has m-1. This avoids fixed coordinate.

Pseudo PG:
```
def projected_gradient_refine(a_in, n, nfft0, nfft_g, rng, deadline, best_a, best_score):
    a = normalize(a_in)
    cur_score = fast_score(a,n,nfft0)
    best_local = a.copy(); best_score_local = cur_score; update global best
    topA = max(8, min(64, n//128))
    step_factor = 1.0
    no_improve = 0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        c = fft_conv(a,n,nfft0)
        cmax = c.max(); score=2*n*cmax
        if finite and score < best_score - 1e-10: update
        if no_improve > 50: # perturb a
            a = perturb(a, rng, sigma=0.05); normalize; no_improve=0
            cur_score=...
            continue
        K = choose active(c, topA, rng? maybe random subset of top? use top)
        g = active_derivative(...)
        d = -g + g.mean()
        maxd=np.max(np.abs(d))
        if maxd<1e-15: break or perturb
        base = a.mean()/maxd
        factors = [0.5*sf,0.25*sf,0.125*sf,0.0625*sf,0.03125*sf]
        improved=False
        for factor in sorted(factors, reverse=True):
            trial = a + factor*base*d
            trial[trial<0]=0
            s=trial.sum(); if s<=eps continue
            trial/=s
            sc=fast_score(trial,n,nfft0)
            if sc < cur_score - 1e-12:
                a=trial; cur_score=sc; improved=True; no_improve=0; break
        if not improved:
            no_improve+=1
            sf *= 0.8 (min 0.1)
```

Let's design move generation:
```
def choose_pair_for_k(a,c,k,n,rng,mode):
    i_lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); i_hi=min(n-1,k)
    # valid i such that j=k-i, choose i<j to avoid dup: i < k-i -> i < k/2
    i_max = min(i_hi, (k-1)//2)  # if i<j
    if i_max < i_lo: return None
    if rng.random()<0.7:
        # choose top product among up to R random samples or full if L small
        L=i_max-i_lo+1
        if L <= 256:
            idxs=np.arange(i_lo,i_max+1); js=k-idxs; prod=a[idxs]*a[js]; t=argmax
        else:
            # sample S=32 random positions, plus maybe global max using np.argmax of segment? Could approximate by top values of a? 
            S=min(64,L); rand_i = i_lo + rng.integers(0,L,size=S); js=k-rand_i; prod=a[rand_i]*a[js]; t=rand_i[argmax]
    else: random one
    i=...; j=k-i; if i>j swap? ensure order; if a[i]==0 or a[j]==0 return None maybe try another.
```

def apply_pair_to_c(c, a, p, q, beta):
    nc = c.copy()
    nc[q:q+na?] += 2*beta*a  # need n
    nc[p:p+n] -= 2*beta*a
    nc[2*q] += beta*beta
    nc[2*p] += beta*beta
    nc[p+q] -= 2*beta*beta
    return nc
```

Thus full local search loop:
- Have a,c,current_score.
- `attempts` since improvement; `pg_counter`.
- While time left:
   - If attempts > e.g. 200 or max edge or rng small: do PG step; recompute c; attempts=0; pg_counter...
   - Else if random < 0.05: do random transfer move (O(n) update) using c; if improve update; else attempts++.

   - After accepted move, update best global (internal) if score < best_score.
   - Every e.g. 2000 moves, recompute c from a via FFT/direct to clean.
- At end, return best.

Let's implement `fast_score` carefully for internal:
```
def fast_score(a, n, nfft):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    s = a.sum()
    if not finite or s < 1e-12: return inf
    a = a/s
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f*f, n=nfft)[:2*n-1]
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m <= 0 or not finite: return inf
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```
For random shape sample, many calls. Could optimize by precomputing x? no. `np.fft` overhead. We can use direct for n small? FFT okay.

For local search with exact c, after PG/recompute we need c. We can recompute via `sequence_convolution(a,n,use_direct)`:

def convolution_for_score(a,n,use_direct=None):
   a=np.asarray...
   if n <= 8000 and (use_direct is None or use_direct):
       c = np.convolve(a,a,'full')
   else:
       nfft=next_power_two(2*n-1); f=rfft; c=irfft...
   c = np.asarray(c,float)
   # clean tiny negative
   if c.min() < 0 and c.min() > -1e-9: c[c<0]=0
   return c
```

Implement local search loop details:
```
def run_local_search(a0, c0, score0, n, nfft, rng, deadline, verbose=True):
    a = normalize(a0)
    c = c0.copy()
    score = score0
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = score
    attempts = 0
    total_moves = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()
    DIRECT_LIMIT = 8000
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       # clean/ recompute if total_moves%5000==0 and total_moves>0
       if total_moves and total_moves % 5000 ==0:
           c = convolution_for_score(a,n,use_fft=True)
           cmax=float(np.max(c)); score=2*n*cmax
           if score < best_score: ...
       # print every 30s
       # decide PG
       k = int(np.argmax(c))
       use_pg = False
       if attempts > 200: use_pg=True
       elif k < n//4 or k > 3*n//4: use_pg=True  # edge
       elif rng.random()<0.01: use_pg=True
       if use_pg:
           a, c, score, imp = pg_step(a,c,score,k,n,nfft,rng)
           if imp:
              update best; attempts=0; total_moves+=1
              continue
           attempts +=1
           if attempts>1000: # big random restart from best
               a = perturb(best_a, rng, 0.1); c=convolve; score=...; if score>best_score? maybe accept even worse for exploration but keep best.
               attempts=0
           continue
       # random transfer with prob 0.1
       if rng.random()<0.1:
           a,c,score,imp = random_transfer_step(a,c,score,n,rng)
           if imp: update; attempts=0; total_moves+=1
           else: attempts+=1
           continue
       # pair balancing
       # choose k random among top? Use if pair possible; else PG
       ok = True
       # if k no pairs, set ok false
       if k <=0 or k >=2*n-2? edges maybe no pairs for k=0,2n-2; for k=1 pair (0,1) exists if n>1. If no pair, use PG.
       # pair step function does selection and tries; returns imp
       a,c,score,imp = pair_step(a,c,score,k,n,rng)
       if imp: update; attempts=0; total_moves+=1
       else: attempts+=1
       if attempts > 500: # try perturbation of best
           a = perturb(best_a, rng, 0.05)
           c=conv; score=...
           attempts=0
           # even if worse, current a worse, but best kept. Could set current to best? Better restart from best plus perturbation.
```

`pg_step` implementation:
```
def pg_step(a, c, score, k, n, nfft, rng):
    # maybe choose k if invalid random max
    g = np.zeros(n)
    i_lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); i_hi=min(n-1,k)
    if i_hi<i_lo: return ...
    r = a[::-1]
    start = n-1-k+i_lo
    L = i_hi-i_lo+1
    g[i_lo:i_hi+1] = r[start:start+L]  # factor irrelevant
    d = -g + g.mean()
    maxd = np.max(np.abs(d)); if small no
    base = (a.mean()/maxd)
    best_trial=None; best_sc=score
    factors = (0.2,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.4,0.8)
    # maybe random factor order? Sort descending but if large overshoots, smaller later. Use [0.2,0.4,0.1,0.05,0.02,0.8]
    for f in factors:
       lam=f*base
       trial = a + lam*d
       if trial.min()<0: trial = np.maximum(trial,0)
       s=trial.sum(); if s<=eps continue
       trial/=s
       sc = fast_score(trial,n,nfft)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
          # compute new c exact/fft
          c_trial = convolution_for_score(trial,n, use_direct=(n<=8000))
          best_trial=trial; best_c=c_trial; best_sc=sc
          # continue to see smaller/larger? We can stop first improvement to save. But maybe larger factor in list better. Could test all and choose best.
    if best_trial is not None:
       return best_trial,best_c,best_sc,True
    return a,c,score,False
```
If we test all factors, up to 6 FFT. Accept best. Good. But if factor order not include optimal, okay. Use factors = [0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5]? base = a.mean()/maxd; d can change all values. 1.5 may be aggressive. Include 0.02. 7 evals. PG not too frequent.

`pair_step`:
```
def pair_step(a,c,score,k,n,rng):
   # choose k maybe if c[k] not finite
   i_lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); i_hi=min(n-1,k)
   i_max=min(i_hi, (k-1)//2)  # i<j
   if i_max < i_lo: return False
   L=i_max-i_lo+1
   # choose pair
   if L <= 64 or rng.random()<0.1:
       idxs=np.arange(i_lo,i_max+1)
       js=k-idxs
       # ensure js>idxs; by i_max
       prod=a[idxs]*a[js]
       t=int(np.argmax(prod))
       i=int(idxs[t]); j=int(js[t])
   else:
       # sample S=32 plus maybe mid
       S=min(32,L)
       sample = i_lo + rng.integers(0,L,S)
       js=k-sample
       prod=a[sample]*a[js]
       t=int(np.argmax(prod))
       i=int(sample[t]); j=int(js[t])
   if i==j or i<0 or j<0 or i>=n or j>=n: return False
   x=a[i]; y=a[j]
   if x < y: i,j = j,i; x,y = y,x  # i larger, j smaller? Let's define large=i, small=j.
   if y <= 1e-18: return False
   # maybe if x/y small? full shift okay.
   # try betas fractions of y
   best=None
   for beta in (0.25*y, 0.5*y, 1.0*y, 0.1*y, 0.75*y): # order? 0.25,0.5,0.1,1 maybe
       if beta<=0: continue
       nc = apply_pair_to_c(c,a,j,i,beta) # p=small, q=large
       sc = 2*n*np.max(nc)
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12: best...
       if sc < score - 1e-12: maybe update current? But we want best; choose best among all and return if improves. Continue all 5.
   if best: update a[small]-=beta, a[large]+=beta; return best
```
We can optimize by computing score after each β and keeping best. If a β improves, smaller? We still test all, 5 copies. Maybe too heavy. Use early accept first improvement? But full β may be better. Could test [0.5,0.25,1.0,0.1,0.75], stop when found? If 0.5 improves, maybe full better but not necessary. To save, stop at first improvement. Greedy with modest step. Use factors [0.25,0.5,0.1,0.75,1.0]. If none, no. Maybe full not needed; 0.5 enough. Let's use 3 factors [0.25,0.5,0.1] to save. If stuck, try 1.0? Could include 1.0 as 4th if no smaller improve. So:
```
for beta in (0.25*y,0.5*y,0.1*y,1.0*y,0.75*y): if improve return
```
Each candidate copies c and two vector adds. 5* maybe okay. Could stop after first.

p,q = rng.integers(0,n,2); if p==q skip

small,large = ... beta = 0.1*min(x,y)
try move small→large and large→small with beta

Perturb function:
```
def perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    b=a.copy()
    n=len(b)
    w=int(max(3, n//100))
    s=int(rng.integers(0,n-w+1))
    b[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,w))
    if b.min()<0: b=np.maximum(b,0)
    ssum=b.sum()
    if ssum>0: b/=ssum
    else return uniform
    return b
```
Could also add global Gaussian. Use when stuck. If perturbing best gives worse, we set current to perturbed for exploration, but best kept. Need ensure not stuck in worse basin; after some time, return to best. In loop, if current_score > best_score*1.1 maybe reset to best.

Global best update inside local search: If score < best_score, set best. At end, return best.

Analytic samples:
```
def make_analytic(n, rng):
   ts=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   funcs=[]
   for alpha in [-1,-0.5,0,0.5,1,2]: funcs.append(1+alpha*ts)
   for alpha in [0.5,1,2,-0.5]: funcs.append(1+alpha*(ts-0.5)**2)
   for beta in [-2,-1,0,1,2]: funcs.append(np.exp(beta*ts))
   for alpha in [0.2,0.5,0.8]: funcs.append(1+alpha*np.cos(2*np.pi*ts))
   for alpha in [0.2,0.5,0.8]: funcs.append(1-alpha*np.cos(2*np.pi*ts))
   for p in [0.5,1,2]: funcs.append(ts**p)
   # ensure positive, scale.
```

Implement `convolution_fft(a,n,nfft)`:
```
def convolution_fft(a,n,nfft):
    f=np.fft.rfft(a,nfft)
    c=np.fft.irfft(f*f,n=n=nfft)
    return c[:2*n-1].astype(np.float64)
```
No direct. For initial, `a` normalized, c max small. Fine.

def _run_local_search(a0, n, nfft, rng, deadline, start_score=None):
    a = _normalize(a0)
    c = _convolution_fft(a,n,nfft)
    score = 2.0*n*float(np.max(c)) if c.size else np.inf
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score=score
    if not np.isfinite(score): ... return
    attempts=0; total=0; last_print=time.monotonic()
    direct? no
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if not np.isfinite(score) or c is None:
           a=best_a.copy(); c=_convolution_fft(a,n,nfft); score=2*n*np.max(c)
        # print every 30s
        now=time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 30:
           print(f"local score {best_score:.6f} current {score:.6f} moves {total}")
           last_print=now
        # cleanup every 20000 moves?
        total+=1 maybe before? Use iterations counter.
        if total>0 and total % 20000 ==0:
           c=_convolution_fft(a,n,nfft); score=2*n*float(np.max(c))
           if score < best_score: best...
        # decide PG
        k=int(np.argmax(c))
        use_pg = (attempts > 300) or (k < max(1,n//3)) or (k > 2*n-2 - max(1,n//3)) or (rng.random()<0.02)
        if use_pg:
           a2,c2,sc2,imp = _pg_step(a,c,score,k,n,nfft,rng)
           if imp:
              a=a2; c=c2; score=sc2; attempts=0; total? 
              if score < best_score: best...
              continue
           attempts +=1
        else:
           if rng.random()<0.08:
              a2,c2,sc2,imp = _random_transfer_step(a,c,score,n,rng)
              ...
              continue
           # pair step; if no pair, pg
           if k <=0 or k >= 2*n-2:
              use_pg=True; continue? Actually k=0 no pair. for k=1 pair exists. condition if no i<j: k<1 or k>2n-3? For k=0,2n-2 no pair.
           a2,c2,sc2,imp = _pair_step(a,c,score,k,n,rng, aggressive=(attempts>100))
           if imp: ... else attempts +=1
        if attempts > 1000:
           # restart from best with perturbation
           a = _perturb_sequence(best_a, rng, 0.15)
           c = _convolution_fft(a,n,nfft); score=2*n*float(np.max(c))
           attempts=0
           # if score much worse, still explore; but maybe reset to best after few.

           a = best_a.copy(); c=_convolution_fft(a,n,nfft); score=best_score; attempts=0
    return best_a,best_score

Let's approximate. f=1+α cos2πt, α≤1. For α=1: f=2cos²πt =1+cos2πt, nonnegative zero at 0.5, high at 0,1. F=1. Convolution? This density has zeros center. Could max be? So center low bad for u=1 because f symmetric and f(t)=f(1-t), product square. Need asymmetry.

What about f(t)=1+α cos(2πt+φ) asymmetric? It may reduce. Hard.

Could use linear f with β optimized gave only 1.93. But local search can do multi-peak.

Let's attempt an analytical construction using **binomial coefficients**? A(z)= (1+z)^m? Coefficients binomial, length m+1. A^2 coefficients binomial(2m). Score? A is conv of m ones? Let's compute normalized discrete. a_i=C(m,i), i=0..m, zero padded to n maybe. S=2^m. Conv c_k=C(2m,k) for k=0..2m. Max C(2m,m) ~ 4^m/√(πm). S^2=4^m. max/S^2 ~1/√(πm). Score=2n /√(πm). If length n≈2m? Score ~2n/√(π n/2)=√(8πn)/? grows with √n, huge. If n=m? zero pad n much bigger? Score huge. Not good.

A(z)=∏(1 - r z)? coefficients geometric alternating? nonnegative if r<1? Actually 1/(1-rz) truncated geometric. We tested exponential.

Maybe optimal A has coefficients following a **sinc-like** function: A(z)= (sin(Nz))/(sin(z)) etc. But nonnegative? Dirichlet kernel has negative coefficients. Not allowed.

Let's think of possible improvements to pair local search: Use **top diagonal not just max but all near max**. We can maintain a set of hot diagonals and choose pair among them to balance. - `top_count = max(16, min(64, n//128))`. Compute `top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -top_count)[-top_count:]` maybe O(n). Then `weights = c[top_idx]`; choose k weighted. This is more expensive but every move O(n) (already argmax O(n); argpartition O(n)). Could do every move, okay. It avoids focusing one diagonal. Use if `rng.random()<0.5`? For simplicity, compute top_idx every e.g. 10 moves or when attempts high. But `np.argmax` cheaper. We can in pair_step choose current k; and if attempts>50 choose from top. `_choose_active_k`:
```
def _choose_active_k(c,n,rng,use_top):
    if not use_top:
       return int(np.argmax(c))
    top = max(16, min(64, n//128))
    m=c.size
    if top>=m: top=m-1
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -top)[-top:]
    w = c[idx].astype(float)
    w = np.maximum(w, 1e-300)
    sw=w.sum()
    if not finite or sw<=0: return int(np.argmax(c))
    return int(idx[int(rng.choice(len(idx), p=w/sw))])
```
`rng.choice` with p. For top 64, okay. `argpartition` maybe O(n). If this every iteration, cost maybe comparable to c max. Fine.

Pair selection: If L large and `use_top_product` maybe sample. To target high product, use full product if L<=256 else sample 32. But for random active k not max, maybe max product. Use random sometimes.

Detailed helper code:

```
def _next_power_two(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()
```

```
def _normalize_sequence(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s
```
Clip huge? If a has inf, where finite false ->0; if all inf -> zeros -> None. Good.

```
def _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft):
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, n=nfft)
    return c[:2*n-1].astype(np.float64, copy=False)
```

```
def _score_from_sequence(a, n, nfft):
    a = _normalize_sequence(a)
    if a is None: return np.inf, None
    c = _fft_convolution(a,n,nfft)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf,a
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m <= 0 or not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf, a
    return float(2.0 * n * m), a
```
But for initial candidates we already normalized; `score_from_sequence` normalizes again, okay. For local we need normalized a and c.

```
def _score_from_c(c,n):
    if c is None or c.size==0: return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if m <= 0 or not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    return float(2.0*n*m)
```

```
def _make_piecewise_sequence(n, m, rng, sigma=1.0, monotone=False):
    ty = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,m)
    if monotone:
        raw = rng.random(m)
        # make monotonically increasing maybe cumulative positive
        steps = np.abs(rng.normal(0,1,m))
        vals = np.cumsum(steps)
        vals[0]=1.0
    else:
        z = rng.normal(0,1,m)
        vals = np.exp(z*sigma)
        # sometimes enforce endpoint patterns? no
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-6)
    tx = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
    a = np.interp(tx, ty, vals)
    return _normalize_sequence(a)
```
For monotone, more elaborate: `vals = 1 + sigma * rng.random(m)` sorted? For increasing: `vals = 1 + sigma * np.sort(rng.random(m))`; decreasing reverse. Good.

```
def _analytic_candidates(n):
   ts=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   arrs=[]
   for alpha in (-1.0,-0.5,0.0,0.5,1.0,2.0):
       arr=1.0+alpha*ts
       arrs.append(arr)
   for alpha in (-0.5,0.5,1.0,2.0):
       arr=1.0+alpha*(2.0*ts-1.0)**2
       arrs.append(arr)
   for beta in (-2.0,-1.0,0.0,1.0,2.0):
       arr=np.exp(beta*ts)
       arrs.append(arr)
   for alpha in (0.25,0.5,0.75):
       arr=1.0+alpha*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*ts)
       arrs.append(arr)
       arr=1.0-alpha*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*ts)
       arrs.append(arr)
   for p in (0.5,1.0,2.0):
       arr=ts**p
       arrs.append(arr)
       arr=(1.0-ts)**p
       arrs.append(arr)
   return arrs
```
Some arrays negative (1+alpha cos with alpha 0.75 min0.25 positive; 1+alpha(2t-1)^2 with alpha -0.5 min0.5 positive; 1+alpha t with alpha -1 min0 at t=1? zero okay; no negative). Good.

`_perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma)`:
```
def _perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    n = a.size
    b = a.copy()
    w = int(max(3, n//100))
    if w < n:
       s = int(rng.integers(0, n-w+1))
       b[s:s+w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, w))
    # global small
    b *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.1*sigma, n))  # maybe O(n)
    b = np.maximum(b,0)
    b = _normalize_sequence(b)
    if b is None:
       b = np.full(n,1.0/n)
    return b
```
Multiplying all by exp could create values >? normalized. Good.

`_apply_pair_to_c(c, a, src, dst, beta, n)`:
```
def _apply_pair_to_c(c, a, src, dst, beta, n):
    nc = c.copy()
    # src decreases by beta, dst increases by beta
    nc[src:src+n] -= 2.0*beta*a
    nc[dst:dst+n] += 2.0*beta*a
    b2 = beta*beta
    nc[2*src] += b2
    nc[2*dst] += b2
    if src != dst:
       nc[src+dst] -= 2.0*b2
    return nc
```
Need indices valid: src,dst 0..n-1; 2*src≤2n-2; src+dst≤2n-2. Slices src:src+n length n; if src > 2n-1-n? max src=n-1 => n-1:2n-1 okay. Good.

`_pair_step`:
```
def _pair_step(a,c,score,k,n,rng,aggressive):
    i_lo = max(0, k-(n-1))
    i_hi = min(n-1, k)
    i_max = min(i_hi, (k-1)//2)  # i<j
    if i_max < i_lo:
        return None,None,score,False
    L = i_max - i_lo +1
    if L <= 96:
        idxs=np.arange(i_lo, i_max+1, dtype=np.int64)
        js=k - idxs
        prod=a[idxs]*a[js]
        t=int(np.argmax(prod))
        i=int(idxs[t]); j=int(js[t])
    else:
        S=min(48,L)
        sample=i_lo + rng.integers(0,L,S)
        js=k - sample
        prod=a[sample]*a[js]
        t=int(np.argmax(prod))
        i=int(sample[t]); j=int(js[t])
        # with prob random pick among samples? Actually argmax. Could if rng<0.3 choose random: t=rng.integers(S)
        if rng.random()<0.25:
            t=int(rng.integers(0,S)); i=int(sample[t]); j=int(js[t])
    if i<0 or j<0 or i>=n or j>=n or i==j:
        return None,None,score,False
    if a[i] < a[j]:
        i,j = j,i
        # Now i larger, j smaller
    small_idx = j
    large_idx = i
    small_val = a[j]
    large_val = a[i]
    if small_val <= 1e-15:
        return None,None,score,False
    best_beta=0; best_c=None; best_sc=score
    if aggressive:
        factors=(0.25,0.5,0.1,1.0,0.75,0.05)
    else:
        factors=(0.25,0.5,0.1)
    for f in factors:
        beta = f*small_val
        if beta <=0: continue
        nc = _apply_pair_to_c(c,a,small_idx,large_idx,beta,n)
        sc = _score_from_c(nc,n)
        if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
            best_sc=sc; best_c=nc; best_beta=beta
            # can break? For aggressive maybe continue to find better; but to save, break after first? Let's continue only up to first improvement? We'll break to save.
            break
    if best_c is None:
        return None,None,score,False
    a2=a.copy()
    a2[small_idx] -= best_beta
    a2[large_idx] += best_beta
    # clean tiny negative
    if a2.min()<0: a2 = np.maximum(a2,0)
    s=a2.sum()
    if s<=0: return None,None,score,False
    a2 /= s
    # renormalize c? Since we normalized a by s close to1. Could scale c by 1/s^2.
    if abs(s-1.0) > 1e-12:
        best_c = best_c / (s*s)
    return a2,best_c,best_sc,True
```
If we normalize a2 slightly, c should scale by 1/s^2 because convolution quadratic. The update c was for a2 before renorm (sum s). If s !=1, c_actual = best_c / s^2. Score from c_actual = best_sc/s²? Wait best_sc computed from best_c for sum s (which may not be exactly1 due rounding). But score formula uses max/S². Our current score variable assumed a normalized S=1. If best_c sum s, score should be 2n max(best_c)/s². `_score_from_c` assumed S=1. If s differs tiny, could use `best_sc /= (s*s)`. But our a2 normalized to 1. Let's compute: New c_raw for unnormalized a_unnorm = a + Δ with sum s. We applied update using a (sum 1) and beta, resulting c_raw. Score true =2n max(c_raw)/s². We set a2=a_unnorm/s, c2=c_raw/s². In code, if s close 1, we can `best_c = best_c/(s*s)` and `best_sc = 2n*max(best_c)`. But best_sc from _score_from_c raw is not normalized. Better compute after scaling:
```

a2=a_unnorm/s
c2=c_raw/(s*s)
sc2=2n*max(c2)
```

def _random_transfer_step(a,c,score,n,rng):

`_pg_step` implementation careful:
```
def _pg_step(a,c,score,k,n,nfft,rng):
    g=np.zeros(n)
    i_lo=max(0,k-(n-1)); i_hi=min(n-1,k)
    if i_hi < i_lo: return False
    r=a[::-1]
    start=n-1-k+i_lo
    L=i_hi-i_lo+1
    g[i_lo:i_hi+1] = r[start:start+L]
    d = -g
    d += g.mean()
    md = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if md <= 1e-15: return False
    base = (float(a.mean())/md)
    # factors
    factors=(0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4,0.8,1.5)
    best_sc=score; best_trial=None; best_c=None
    for f in factors:
       lam=f*base
       trial=a + lam*d
       if trial.min()<0: trial=np.maximum(trial,0)
       s=trial.sum()
       if s<=1e-15: continue
       trial=trial/s
       sc, _ = _score_from_sequence(trial,n,nfft)  # This normalizes again (already) and computes c
       if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
           best_sc=sc; best_trial=trial
           # We need c for accepted; can compute later.
    if best_trial is None: return None,None,score,False
    best_c = _fft_convolution(best_trial,n,nfft)
    sc = _score_from_c(best_c,n)
    # due FFT, sc may differ; if not finite or worse significantly, reject? Use sc.
    if not np.isfinite(sc) or sc >= score - 1e-12:
       return None,None,score,False
    return best_trial,best_c,sc,True
```
Calling `_score_from_sequence` normalizes and computes c, but we didn't return c. Could compute c in that function and return. Simpler have `_score_and_conv(trial,n,nfft)`:
```
def _score_and_conv(a,n,nfft):
   a=_normalize_sequence(a)
   c=_fft_convolution(a,n,nfft); sc=...
   return a,c,sc
```

```
def _choose_active_k(c,n,rng,use_top):
    if not use_top:
       return int(np.argmax(c))
    top = max(8, min(64, max(8, n//64)))
    m=c.size
    if top >= m:
       top = max(1, m-1)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -top)[-top:]
    w = np.asarray(c[idx], dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w) & (w>0), w, 1e-300)
    sw = w.sum()
    if sw <=0 or not finite:
       return int(np.argmax(c))
    return int(idx[int(rng.choice(idx.size, p=w/sw))])
```
For weighted choice, `idx` unsorted; okay. If top small, probability ∝ c (high peak). Good.

def _scale_for_eval(a, cap=999.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size==0: return [1.0]
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,None)
    m=float(a.max())
    if m <= 0 or not finite: return [1.0]*a.size? But length matters; use [1.0]*len(a)
    out = a*(cap/m)
    out = np.clip(out,0.0,1000.0)
    # if sum too small? cap/m large, sum ~cap/minmax; if all values normal sum>0.01.
    return [float(x) for x in out]
```
Use cap=999.0 to avoid clipping. If m huge? normalized m≤1, cap/m≥999, sum maybe huge. Good.

def _resample_normalized(a,n2):
   a=np.asarray(a)
   if a.size==0: None
   x=np.linspace(0,1,a.size)
   y=np.linspace(0,1,n2)
   b=np.interp(y,x,a)
   return _normalize_sequence(b)
```

```python
"""
Search nonnegative step-function heights minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)**2).

The implementation combines:
  1. Rapid FFT-based evaluation of many normalized candidates.
  2. A greedy exact local search using one-dimensional convolution updates:
     for a currently hot anti-diagonal, move mass along a pair on that
     anti-diagonal and accept only if the exact convolution L-infinity target
     improves.
  3. Periodic projected-gradient steps in convolution space to handle edge
     anti-diagonals and smoother basin moves.
  4. Random restarts / block perturbations to escape local minima.
Finally, the best normalized shape is scaled below the evaluator's 1000 cap.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_power_two(x):
    x = int(max(1, x))
    return 1 << (x - 1).bit_length()


def _prepare_normalized(arr, n):
    """Normalize a candidate to nonnegative sum-one, resampling if needed."""
    a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = int(n)
    if n <= 0:
        return None
    if a.size == 0:
        return None
    if a.size == 1:
        v = float(a[0])
        if not np.isfinite(v) or v <= 0.0:
            return None
        return np.full(n, v / (n * v), dtype=np.float64)
    if a.size != n:
        x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, endpoint=True)
        y = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=True)
        a = np.interp(y, x, a)
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(a))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return None
    a = a / m
    s = float(np.sum(a))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    return a / s


def _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft):
    f = np.fft.rfft(a, nfft)
    c = np.fft.irfft(f * f, n=nfft)
    return c[:2 * n - 1].astype(np.float64, copy=False)


def _score_from_c(c, n):
    if c is None or c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return inf = float("inf")
        # unreachable, keeps static analyzers quiet
    return float(2.0 * n * m)


def _score_and_conv(a, n, nfft):
    a = _prepare_normalized(a, n)
    if a is None:
        return None, None, np.inf
    c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
    s = _score_from_c(c, n)
    return a, c, s


def _analytic_candidates_tx(tx):
    ts = tx
    out = []

    for alpha in (-1.0, -0.5, 0.0, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        out.append(1.0 + alpha * ts)

    for alpha in (-0.5, 0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        out.append(1.0 + alpha * (2.0 * ts - 1.0) ** 2)

    for beta in (-2.0, -1.0, 0.0, 1.0, 2.0):
        out.append(np.exp(beta * ts))

    for alpha in (0.25, 0.5, 0.75):
        out.append(1.0 + alpha * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * ts))
        out.append(1.0 - alpha * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * ts))

    for p in (0.5, 1.0, 2.0):
        out.append(ts ** p)
        out.append((1.0 - ts) ** p)

    return out


def _make_piecewise_sequence(n, tx, m, rng, sigma, monotone):
    m = int(max(3, min(m, 64)))
    ty = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, endpoint=True)
    if monotone:
        steps = np.abs(rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=m))
        vals = 1.0 + sigma * np.cumsum(steps)
        if rng.random() < 0.5:
            vals = vals[::-1]
    else:
        z = rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, size=m)
        vals = np.exp(z * sigma)
    vals = np.maximum(vals, 1e-6)
    a = np.interp(tx, ty, vals)
    return _prepare_normalized(a, n)


def _perturb_sequence(a, rng, sigma):
    n = a.size
    b = a.astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    w = int(max(3, n // 100))
    if w < n:
        s = int(rng.integers(0, n - w + 1))
        b[s:s + w] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=w))
    global_scale = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.1 * sigma))
    b *= global_scale
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    b = _prepare_normalized(b, n)
    if b is None:
        b = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
    return b


def _scale_for_eval(a, cap=999.0):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return [1.0]
    a = np.where(np.isfinite(a), a, 0.0)
    a = np.maximum(a, 0.0)
    m = float(np.max(a))
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return [1.0] * a.size
    out = a * (float(cap) / m)
    out = np.clip(out, 0.0, 1000.0)
    return [float(x) for x in out]


def _resample_normalized(a, n2):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n2 = int(n2)
    if a.size == 0 or n2 <= 0:
        return None
    if a.size == 1:
        v = float(a[0])
        if not np.isfinite(v) or v <= 0.0:
            return None
        return np.full(n2, v / (n2 * v), dtype=np.float64)
    x = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, endpoint=True)
    y = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n2, endpoint=True)
    b = np.interp(y, x, a)
    return _prepare_normalized(b, n2)


def _apply_pair_to_c(c, a, src, dst, beta, n):
    """
    Update convolution c after a[src] -> a[src]-beta, a[dst] -> a[dst]+beta.
    Exact for full convolution of a with itself.
    """
    nc = c.copy()
    nc[src:src + n] -= 2.0 * beta * a
    nc[dst:dst + n] += 2.0 * beta * a
    b2 = beta * beta
    nc[2 * src] += b2
    nc[2 * dst] += b2
    if src != dst:
        nc[src + dst] -= 2.0 * b2
    return nc


def _choose_active_k(c, n, rng, use_top):
    if not use_top:
        return int(np.argmax(c))
    top = max(8, min(64, max(8, n // 64)))
    m = c.size
    if top >= m:
        top = max(1, m - 1)
    idx = np.argpartition(c, -top)[-top:]
    w = np.asarray(c[idx], dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.where(np.isfinite(w) & (w > 0.0), w, 1e-300)
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= 0.0:
        return int(np.argmax(c))
    w = w / sw
    return int(idx[int(rng.choice(idx.size, p=w))])


def _pair_step(a, c, score, k, n, rng, aggressive):
    """Greedy move along one anti-diagonal k, trying several mass shifts."""
    i_lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
    i_hi = min(n - 1, k)
    i_max = min(i_hi, (k - 1) // 2)  # enforce i < j
    if i_max < i_lo:
        return None, None, score, False

    L = i_max - i_lo + 1
    if L <= 96:
        idxs = np.arange(i_lo, i_max + 1, dtype=np.int64)
        js = k - idxs
        prod = a[idxs] * a[js]
        t = int(np.argmax(prod))
        i = int(idxs[t])
        j = int(js[t])
    else:
        S = min(48, L)
        sample = i_lo + rng.integers(0, L, S)
        js = k - sample
        prod = a[sample] * a[js]
        # Occasionally take a random sample instead of the best sampled pair.
        if rng.random() < 0.25:
            t = int(rng.integers(0, S))
        else:
            t = int(np.argmax(prod))
        i = int(sample[t])
        j = int(js[t])

    if i < 0 or j < 0 or i >= n or j >= n or i == j:
        return None, None, score, False

    # i will be the larger one, j the smaller one.
    if a[i] < a[j]:
        i, j = j, i

    large_idx = i
    small_idx = j
    small_val = a[small_idx]
    large_val = a[large_idx]

    best_c = None
    best_sc = score
    best_move = None

    # Forward skew: move mass from smaller to larger (usually reduces this diagonal).
    if small_val > 1e-15:
        if aggressive:
            factors = (0.25, 0.5, 0.1, 1.0, 0.75, 0.05)
        else:
            factors = (0.25, 0.5, 0.1)
        for f in factors:
            beta = f * small_val
            if beta <= 0.0:
                continue
            nc = _apply_pair_to_c(c, a, small_idx, large_idx, beta, n)
            sc = _score_from_c(nc, n)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_c = nc
                best_sc = sc
                best_move = (small_idx, large_idx, beta)
                break

    # Reverse skew: spread mass from larger to smaller (may reduce edge peaks).
    if best_move is None and large_val > 1e-15:
        for f in (0.05, 0.1, 0.2):
            beta = f * large_val
            if beta <= 0.0:
                continue
            nc = _apply_pair_to_c(c, a, large_idx, small_idx, beta, n)
            sc = _score_from_c(nc, n)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_c = nc
                best_sc = sc
                best_move = (large_idx, small_idx, beta)
                break

    if best_move is None:
        return None, None, score, False

    src, dst, beta = best_move
    a_raw = a.copy()
    a_raw[src] -= beta
    a_raw[dst] += beta
    a_raw = np.maximum(a_raw, 0.0)
    s_raw = float(np.sum(a_raw))
    if not np.isfinite(s_raw) or s_raw <= 0.0:
        return None, None, score, False

    a_new = a_raw / s_raw
    c_new = best_c / (s_raw * s_raw)
    sc_new = _score_from_c(c_new, n)
    if not np.isfinite(sc_new) or sc_new >= score - 1e-12:
        return None, None, score, False
    return a_new, c_new, float(sc_new), True


def _random_transfer_step(a, c, score, n, rng):
    """Random small mass transfer between two coordinates, both directions."""
    p = int(rng.integers(0, n))
    q = int(rng.integers(0, n))
    if p == q:
        return None, None, score, False

    best_c = None
    best_sc = score
    best_move = None

    for src, dst in ((p, q), (q, p)):
        base = a[src]
        if base <= 1e-15:
            continue
        for f in (0.03, 0.1, 0.25):
            beta = f * base
            if beta <= 0.0:
                continue
            nc = _apply_pair_to_c(c, a, src, dst, beta, n)
            sc = _score_from_c(nc, n)
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_c = nc
                best_sc = sc
                best_move = (src, dst, beta)

    if best_move is None:
        return None, None, score, False

    src, dst, beta = best_move
    a_raw = a.copy()
    a_raw[src] -= beta
    a_raw[dst] += beta
    a_raw = np.maximum(a_raw, 0.0)
    s_raw = float(np.sum(a_raw))
    if not np.isfinite(s_raw) or s_raw <= 0.0:
        return None, None, score, False

    a_new = a_raw / s_raw
    c_new = best_c / (s_raw * s_raw)
    sc_new = _score_from_c(c_new, n)
    if not np.isfinite(sc_new) or sc_new >= score - 1e-12:
        return None, None, score, False
    return a_new, c_new, float(sc_new), True


def _pg_step(a, c, score, k, n, nfft, rng):
    """
    Projected-gradient step reducing the chosen convolution anti-diagonal k
    while preserving sum. Fallback for edge peaks and smoother basin moves.
    """
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    i_lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
    i_hi = min(n - 1, k)
    if i_hi < i_lo:
        return None, None, score, False

    r = a[::-1]
    start = n - 1 - k + i_lo
    L = i_hi - i_lo + 1
    g[i_lo:i_hi + 1] = r[start:start + L]

    d = -g
    d += float(np.mean(g))
    md = float(np.max(np.abs(d)))
    if not np.isfinite(md) or md <= 1e-15:
        return None, None, score, False

    base = max(1e-30, float(np.mean(a)) / md)
    factors = (0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4, 0.8, 1.5)

    best_a = None
    best_bound = score
    for f in factors:
        lam = f * base
        trial = a + lam * d
        if np.min(trial) < 0.0:
            trial = np.maximum(trial, 0.0)
        a_trial = _prepare_normalized(trial, n)
        if a_trial is None:
            continue
        _, _, sc = _score_and_conv(a_trial, n, nfft)
        if sc < best_bound - 1e-12:
            best_bound = sc
            best_a = a_trial

    if best_a is None:
        return None, None, score, False

    c_new = _fft_convolution(best_a, n, nfft)
    sc_new = _score_from_c(c_new, n)
    if not np.isfinite(sc_new) or sc_new >= score - 1e-12:
        return None, None, score, False
    return best_a, c_new, float(sc_new), True


def _run_local_search(a0, n, nfft, rng, deadline):
    """Local exact/FFT hybrid search. Returns (best_normalized_a, best_score)."""
    a, c, score = _score_and_conv(a0, n, nfft)
    if a is None:
        a = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
        score = _score_from_c(c, n)

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = float(score)

    attempts = 0
    iters = 0
    last_print = time.monotonic()

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        iters += 1

        # Periodically recompute convolution from FFT to remove roundoff drift.
        if iters % 20000 == 0:
            c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
            score = _score_from_c(c, n)
            if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = float(score)
                best_a = a.copy()

        now = time.monotonic()
        if now - last_print > 30.0:
            print(
                "local search iters={} best={:.6f} current={:.6f}".format(
                    iters, best_score, score if np.isfinite(score) else float("inf")
                ),
                flush=True,
            )
            last_print = now

        if not np.isfinite(score) or c is None:
            a = best_a.copy()
            c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
            score = float(best_score)

        use_top = attempts > 20 or rng.random() < 0.35
        k = _choose_active_k(c, n, rng, use_top)

        i_lo = max(0, k - (n - 1))
        i_hi = min(n - 1, k)
        pair_possible = (min(i_hi, (k - 1) // 2) >= i_lo)
        edge_margin = max(2, n // 8)
        near_edge = (k < edge_margin) or (k > (2 * n - 2) - edge_margin)

        if (not pair_possible) or near_edge or attempts > 600 or rng.random() < 0.03:
            a2, c2, sc2, imp = _pg_step(a, c, score, k, n, nfft, rng)
            if imp:
                a = a2
                c = c2
                score = sc2
                attempts = 0
                if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = float(score)
                    best_a = a.copy()
                continue
            attempts += 1
        else:
            if rng.random() < 0.10:
                a2, c2, sc2, imp = _random_transfer_step(a, c, score, n, rng)
                if imp:
                    a = a2
                    c = c2
                    score = sc2
                    attempts = 0
                    if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_score = float(score)
                        best_a = a.copy()
                    continue
                attempts += 1
                continue

            a2, c2, sc2, imp = _pair_step(a, c, score, k, n, rng, aggressive=(attempts > 80))
            if imp:
                a = a2
                c = c2
                score = sc2
                attempts = 0
                if score < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = float(score)
                    best_a = a.copy()
            else:
                attempts += 1

        if attempts > 1500:
            a = _perturb_sequence(best_a, rng, sigma=0.20)
            c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
            score = _score_from_c(c, n)
            attempts = 0

        if attempts > 4000:
            a = best_a.copy()
            c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
            score = float(best_score)
            attempts = 0

    return best_a, float(best_score)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=2400, **kwargs):
    """Return a list of nonnegative heights with low evaluation score."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    t_start = time.monotonic()
    budget = max(0.0, float(budget_s))

    parent = kwargs.get("height_sequence_1", None)
    if parent is None:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)

    parent_ok = False
    parent_len = 0
    if parent is not None:
        try:
            parent_arr_tmp = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if parent_arr_tmp.size > 0:
                parent_len = int(parent_arr_tmp.size)
                parent_ok = True
        except Exception:
            parent_ok = False

    if parent_ok and 1024 <= parent_len <= 20000:
        n = parent_len
    elif parent_ok:
        n = 8192
    else:
        n = 6733

    if n < 1024:
        n = 4096
    if n > 20000:
        n = 16384

    nfft = _next_power_two(2 * n - 1)
    tx = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n, endpoint=True)

    if budget <= 5.0:
        return _scale_for_eval(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), cap=999.0)

    final_margin = max(5.0, min(60.0, 0.05 * budget + 5.0))
    deadline = t_start + budget - final_margin
    if deadline <= t_start:
        deadline = t_start

    best_a = None
    best_score = np.inf

    def record_candidate(arr):
        nonlocal best_a, best_score
        a = _prepare_normalized(arr, n)
        if a is None:
            return
        c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
        sc = _score_from_c(c, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_a = a.copy()

    # Always keep uniform as a valid fallback.
    record_candidate(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    # Parent (different length resampled if necessary).
    if parent_ok:
        record_candidate(parent)

    # Analytic shapes.
    for arr in _analytic_candidates_tx(tx):
        record_candidate(arr)

    # Random low-dimensional smooth shapes.
    random_budget = min(25.0, max(0.0, 0.03 * budget))
    random_end = t_start + random_budget
    m_cycle = [5, 7, 10, 14]
    idx = 0
    while time.monotonic() < random_end:
        m = m_cycle[idx % len(m_cycle)]
        idx += 1
        sigma = float(rng.choice((0.45, 0.8, 1.1, 1.6)))
        mono = bool(rng.random() < 0.25)
        a = _make_piecewise_sequence(n, tx, m, rng, sigma, mono)
        if a is None:
            continue
        c = _fft_convolution(a, n, nfft)
        sc = _score_from_c(c, n)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = float(sc)
            best_a = a.copy()

    if best_a is None:
        best_a = np.full(n, 1.0 / float(n), dtype=np.float64)
        best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(_scale_for_eval(best_a, cap=999.0)))

    print(
        "initial constructive score={:.6f} n={} budget={:.1f}".format(
            best_score, n, budget
        ),
        flush=True,
    )

    refined_a, refined_score = _run_local_search(best_a, n, nfft, rng, deadline)
    if refined_a is not None and np.isfinite(refined_score):
        best_a = refined_a
        best_score = float(refined_score)

    print("local search best internal score={:.6f}".format(best_score), flush=True)

    # Final exact evaluation of the best normalized shape and a few variants.
    final_best = None
    final_best_score = np.inf
    t_hard = t_start + budget - 2.0

    def exact_record(a_norm):
        nonlocal final_best, final_best_score
        if a_norm is None:
            return
        if time.monotonic() > t_hard:
            return
        try:
            lst = _scale_for_eval(a_norm, cap=999.0)
            sc = float(evaluate_sequence(lst))
        except Exception:
            return
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < final_best_score:
            final_best_score = sc
            final_best = lst

    exact_record(best_a)
    exact_record(best_a[::-1])  # Usually same by reversal symmetry, but harmless.

    # Try a couple of nearby lengths if time allows.
    alt_lengths = []
    if n < 8192:
        alt_lengths.append(8192)
    else:
        alt_lengths.append(max(1024, n // 2))
    # Do not duplicate current length.
    unique_alt = []
    for x in alt_lengths:
        if x != n and x not in unique_alt:
            unique_alt.append(int(x))

    for n2 in unique_alt:
        if time.monotonic() > t_hard - 3.0:
            break
        a2 = _resample_normalized(best_a, n2)
        if a2 is not None:
            exact_record(a2)

    if final_best is None or not np.isfinite(final_best_score):
        # Robust fallback: uniform sequence of the chosen length.
        final_best = _scale_for_eval(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64), cap=999.0)

    return final_best
```
